```markdown
# COMPASS: CommonMark

## Compass
Build a command-line parser that converts CommonMark 0.31.2 Markdown to HTML. It serves callers that provide Markdown on standard input and consume HTML on standard output. The deliverable is an executable named `commonmark` at the application root.

## Constraints
- Read UTF-8 Markdown from standard input and write HTML to standard output.
- Follow the supplied CommonMark 0.31.2 specification and parsing strategy.
- Keep the supplied conformance assets under `sources/` unchanged.

## Guardrails
- Do not use a public Markdown implementation as the parser.
- Do not modify the supplied scoring assets; changes are reported as tampering.
- Do not author or replace `sources/full_test.sh`.
- Run the full supplied suite only in the terminal verification story.
```

```markdown
# Technology Stack

**Approved:** 2026-08-10

Technology decisions of record for this Target. One row per technology, naming the
Rigging best-practice file that governs building it.

A `—` in the Rigging column means no Rigging guidance exists for that technology; the
builder applies general best practice instead. Adding a row never requires a matching
Rigging file.

This file is owned by the UAT kit. It is seeded into the Target before `analyze`, which
never overwrites it, and `drydock plan` reads it to assign per-story `stack:` guidance.

| Technology | Rigging | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Python 3 | python.md | Implementation language. |
| Python standard library | common.md | No third-party runtime dependencies. |
| POSIX shell | common.md | `full_test.sh` is required to be POSIX-compatible. |

## Foundation Contract

- source_root: ./
- test_command: pytest -q
- environment: `.venv`, created by `uv venv` and activated by launchers in `bin/`
- dependencies: `pyproject.toml` and committed `uv.lock`

The language foundation owns the project root, environment, dependency manifest, and test runner.
Later foundations add their packages to this tree without replacing those contracts.

### How do we execute and measure Acceptance Criteria in this language?

An Acceptance Criterion (AC) is an executable check of Blueprint-required behavior. Use the
existing Programmatic Acceptance format: a Python script with assertions against the built
product. Drydock executes each criterion through `languages/python/acceptance/runner.py`; `pytest -q`
runs the project test suite. Retain the criterion ID, observed values, stdout/stderr and result;
an assertion failure fails the check. The harness reports failure operands and available case
counts. Code coverage and persistence interactions require explicit instrumentation; record
available observations as key/value data associated with the criterion. Missing observations
are unknown, not proof that code or an interaction was unused.

### How does this language provide the shared persistence contract?

Use `stack/persistence.md`. Drydock copies `Rigging/languages/python/persistence/{db,sqlite,postgres}.py` and generates
`persistence/{schema,__init__,testing}.py` plus `schema.sql` from `DATABASE.md`. Call
`open_database(dsn)` and `db.<table>.<operation>(...)`; use `persistence.testing.db_session()`
for criteria. SQLite uses the standard library; PostgreSQL needs `psycopg[binary]` in the project
dependencies. Both engines ship together and the DSN selects the engine. Library verification
lives in `tests/test_shipped_database.py`; a Target still verifies its own schema and operations.
```
</pblock>

```markdown
## Planning Instructions

### Delivery Shape

Build a command-line Markdown-to-HTML parser. It consumes UTF-8 Markdown on standard input and emits HTML on standard output through the root executable `commonmark`. The supplied specification and harness are staged under `sources/`. The implementation flow is block parsing, link reference collection, inline parsing, then HTML rendering. Scoped stories run their section examples; one terminal story runs the complete unfiltered suite.

| BLOCKS-007 | List items; lists and tightness | `sources/spec.txt` sections “List items” and “Lists” | `sources/spec_tests.py` | Container parsing; scoped verification |
| INLINE-001 | Backslash escapes; entity and numeric character references | `sources/spec.txt` sections “Backslash escapes” and “Entity and numeric character references” | `sources/spec_tests.py` | Inline parsing; scoped verification |

| INLINE-004 | Inline and reference links | `sources/spec.txt` section “Links” | `sources/spec_tests.py` | Inline parsing and reference-map integration; scoped verification |
| INLINE-005 | Images | `sources/spec.txt` section “Images” | `sources/spec_tests.py` | Inline parsing; scoped verification |

| CONFORMANCE-002 | Complete, unfiltered suite verification | `sources/INSTRUCTIONS.md`; `sources/full_test.sh` | All implementation stories; all staged runtime assets | Terminal acceptance contract; full suite only |

### Test and Acceptance Strategy

Run `sources/spec_tests.py` with explicit section selectors for each parser story's complete scope. The selectors must name example-owning headings. Do not invoke `sources/full_test.sh` from scoped checks. CONFORMANCE-002 is the sole terminal verification story, depends on every parser implementation story, and runs `sh sources/full_test.sh` with `Suite: full`. Its assertion checks only for a zero return code and prints captured standard output and standard error.

### Sequencing and Dependencies

Implement block parsing before inline parsing. Complete reference-definition collection during block parsing so inline links can resolve document-wide references. The executable and README provide the callable interface. The terminal suite verification follows all parser implementation stories. Stage `spec_tests.py`, `cmark.py`, `normalize.py`, `spec.txt`, and `full_test.sh` verbatim; do not modify the supplied scoring assets.

### Source Conflicts and Gaps

None. The unattended UAT policy resolves analysis ambiguity without emitting questions or blockers.
```
